reasoning
Since CUTLASS is not available in the environment (cutlass/ header not found), I need to write a custom CUDA kernel for grouped GEMM on H100, using warp-level matrix multiply-accumulate instructions (mma.sync.aligned.m16n8k16) with FP16 inputs and FP32 accumulation. This is a grouped GEMM where each group is independent, with fixed dimensions M, N, K per group. For simplicity and performance on H100, I can exploit the following: - Groups = 15, each M = N = K = 256. - Each group is small enough that a threadblock-level kernel doable. - H100 has 228 KB SMEM and massive tensor-core throughput. - Use a kernel where each threadblock handles a (M_tile, N_tile) of all groups, or each SM handles one group with many warps. Actually, 256 = 16 * 16, so each group can be decomposed into 16x16=256 tiles of 16x16 using m16n8k16 instructions. But to fill H100 SMs, we need many threadblocks. One design: each threadblock computes one 128x128 tile for one group. M = 256, N = 256, so there are (256/128)*(256/128)=4 tiles per group. With 15 groups, that's 60 tiles. That's too few to saturate H100 (132 SMs). But H100 can run multiple threadblocks per SM, and 60 tiles * maybe 2 waves = okay. Better: each threadblock computes a 64x64 or 128x64 tile. Let's aim for 64x64 tiles to get more parallelism. (256/64)*(256/64)=16 tiles per group * 15 groups = 240 tiles. With 128 or 132 SMs, that's about 2 tiles per SM, enough. But the kernel can also be designed so each threadblock computes an entire group of 256x256, but that needs 4 warps per tile * 4 tiles = 16 warps, maybe feasible. Actually, a threadblock of 256 threads (8 warps) can compute a 128x128 tile with 64x64 warp tiles. That's classic. For H100 optimization, we should: - Use mma.sync.aligned.m16n8k16.row.col.f16.f16.f16.f32 or similar (Hopper supports m64n8k16? Actually Hopper warp group mma is wgmma, but m16n8k16 still works). Let's use the basic mma instruction available on both Ampere and Hopper: mma.sync.aligned.m16n8k16.row.col.f16.f16.f16.f32 (A row-major, B col-major). Actually for performance, we might want mma.m16n8k16 with f16 accumulation? No, we need fp32 accumulation for correctness. I'll write a custom kernel: - Data: A [groups, M, K] fp16 row-major, B [groups, K, N] fp16 row-major (so B is row-major too). But mma expects A row-major, B col-major. For row-major B, B col-major would be transposed. We can just read B in a transposed fashion or store B as col-major. In the model __init__, I can store weight as [groups, N, K] or [groups, K, N] and adjust. Wait, in `get_inputs()` we get `torch.randn(GROUPS, M, K)` as A. In `get_init_inputs()` we get `[GROUPS, K, N, ALPHA, BETA]`. So B shape is [GROUPS, K, N]. If I want B to be col-major for mma, I can transpose B in init: store as [groups, N, K] and treat it as col-major with leading dimension K. That's effectively the same memory layout as row-major [groups, K, N]. Let's think: - Row-major [groups, K, N] with ld = N: element (k, n) is at k*N + n. - Col-major [groups, N, K] with ld = N: element (n, k) is at k*N + n. So the memory layout is identical if we view the tensor as transposed. So in the kernel, I can treat A as row-major [M, K], B as col-major [N, K] (i.e., its transpose is row-major [K, N]). The kernel: - Each threadblock handles a 128x128 tile across all groups? Or per group? - To maximize parallelism, assign one threadblock to one (group, tile_m, tile_n). - Threadblock grid: dim3(n_tiles_n, n_tiles_m, groups). For M=N=256, tile=128, that's (2,2,15)=60 blocks. - Each threadblock has 256 threads (8 warps). Each warp computes a 64x64 tile, or maybe each warp computes a 32x64 tile. With 8 warps we can do 2x4 grid of 32x32 tiles inside 128x128? For B: - Thread tid handles indices [tid * 32, tid * 32 + 31) in linearized SMEM_B. - SMEM_B is K_chunk x N_tile col-major, linear index `idx = k + n*K_chunk`. - n = idx / 64, k = idx % 64. - GMEM_B is [N, K] row-major. Element (n, k) is at (n0 + n) * K + (k0 + k). - Consecutive idx mean consecutive k for same n, then next n. In GMEM, this is also consecutive for same n. So coalesced. Great. Now the kernel: Global kernel: ```cuda __global__ void grouped_gemm_wmma( const half* __restrict__ A, const half* __restrict__ B, const half* __restrict__ C, half* __restrict__ D, int M, int N, int K, float alpha, float beta, int groups) { int group = blockIdx.z; int tile_m = blockIdx.y; int tile_n = blockIdx.x; int m_offset = tile_m * 64; int n_offset = tile_n * 64; const half* A_base = A + group * M * K; const half* B_base = B + group * N * K; // B is [N, K] const half* C_base = C + group * M * N; half* D_base = D + group * M * N; __shared__ half smem_A[64 * 64]; __shared__ half smem_B[64 * 64]; float acc[4][4]; // 4x4 wmma tiles of 16x16 each per warp? Let's figure out. ``` Wait, each warp handles a 32x32 area. A 32x32 area has (32/16)*(32/16) = 4 WMMA tiles. A threadblock of 4 warps handles 64x64. Warp 0: m=0..31, n=0..31 Warp 1: m=0..31, n=32..63 Warp 2: m=32..63, n=0..31 Warp 3: m=32..63, n=32..63 Within a warp, 32 threads compute 4 WMMA tiles. Each WMMA tile is 16x16. So: - Tile 0: m=0..15, n=0..15 (relative to warp) - Tile 1: m=0..15, n=16..31 - Tile 2: m=16..31, n=0..15 - Tile 3: m=16..31, n=16..31 Actually, WMMA only operates at the warp level, so each `mma_sync` call is executed by all 32 threads in the warp simultaneously. We can have a loop over the 4 tiles. But wait, `wmma::fragment` is defined per-thread but conceptually holds the warp's data. We declare: ```cuda wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_a, 16, 16, 16, half, wmma::row_major> frag_a; wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_b, 16, 16, 16, half, wmma::col_major> frag_b; wmma::fragment<wmma::accumulator, 16, 16, 16, float> frag_c[4]; ``` Initialize `frag_c` to 0. For k_step = 0..3 (K=256, chunk=64): Load A_smem, B_smem. __syncthreads(); For each wmma tile i=0..3: Compute offset in smem for A and B. wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_a, smem_A + a_offset, 64); wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_b, smem_B + b_offset, 64); wmma::mma_sync(frag_c[i], frag_a, frag_b, frag_c[i]); __syncthreads(); After K loop, we have 4 accumulator fragments in each warp. Now we need to store them to D. For `wmma::store_matrix_sync`, we need to pass a pointer in SMEM or GMEM? It can store directly to GMEM. The store function for accumulator stores all 16x16 elements from the fragment to memory in row-major order. For tile i, the base address in D should be: D + (warp_m_offset + tile_m_within_warp) * N + (warp_n_offset + tile_n_within_warp) Let's compute offsets: - Warp id within block: `warp_id = threadIdx.x / 32` - `warp_m = warp_id / 2` (0 or 1) - `warp_n = warp_id % 2` (0 or 1) - `warp_m_offset = m_offset + warp_m * 32` - `warp_n_offset = n_offset + warp_n * 32` Within warp, wmma tiles: - `wmma_m = (i / 2) * 16` (0 or 16) - `wmma_n = (i % 2) * 16` (0 or 16) So store address: `D_base + (warp_m_offset + wmma_m) * N + (warp_n_offset + wmma_n)`. Leading dimension for store is N. `wmma::store_matrix_sync(D_ptr, frag_c[i], N, wmma::mem_row_major);` But we need to apply alpha and beta! The formula is D = alpha * (A*B) + beta * C. We can either: 1. Load C into accumulators with beta scaling, then do mma and scale A*B by alpha. 2. Or, compute A*B in FP32 accumulators, then after mma, manually load C, scale, and add. WMMA `mma_sync` does D = A*B + C, where C is the accumulator fragment. So we can initialize the accumulator with beta*C and then mma adds A*B. But we also need alpha scaling. We can scale A or B during loading? No, wmma doesn't support scaling. Simplest: initialize accumulators to 0, do mma. After the loop, load C fragment, scale by beta, add to accumulator, then scale accumulator by alpha? Actually D = alpha * (A*B) + beta * C. - Compute A*B -> acc - For each element: acc = alpha * acc + beta * C. Since the output is f16, and we want FP32 accumulation, we can read C from GMEM as f16, convert to float, multiply by beta, add to acc (which is float), then convert to f16 and store. But we can also read C using wmma into a fragment. However, C is not a matrix_a or matrix_b fragment, it's just data. We can use `wmma::load_matrix_sync` with an accumulator fragment? No, accumulator fragments can only be loaded from memory? Actually, there's no load for accumulator type except mma output and store. Wait, on newer CUDA, there might be `wmma::load_matrix_sync` for accumulator? I think not. The WMMA API for accumulator only supports `fill_fragment`, `mma_sync`, and `store_matrix_sync`. So we have to manually read C from GMEM, modify the accumulator, then store. After the K loop, we have `frag_c[i]` containing the A*B result in FP32. We then need to read the corresponding C tile, multiply by beta, add to frag_c, then multiply by alpha, then store to D. But `frag_c[i]` is stored per-thread in registers (an array of floats). The mapping of elements to threads is defined by WMMA. To do elementwise operations on the fragment, we can iterate over `frag_c[i].x[k]` for k=0..frag_c[i].num_elements-1. `num_elements` is the number of elements each thread holds, which for m16n16k16 accumulator is 8 (since 16*16/32 = 8 floats per thread). This is easier! SMEM temporary: We can reuse smem_A or smem_B as a scratchpad. ```cuda __shared__ half smem_D[64 * 64]; // reuse one of the buffers ``` Wait, we have 64KB limit easily. Alternatively, we can store the FP32 accumulator directly to GMEM_D (since D is our output), but we need C too. So: 1. After K loop, store acc to SMEM float buffer (64x64 floats = 16KB). 2. Kernel structure: ```cuda __global__ void grouped_gemm_wmma( const half* __restrict__ A, // [groups, M, K] const half* __restrict__ B, // [groups, N, K] (transposed weight) const half* __restrict__ C, // [groups, M, N] half* __restrict__ D, int M, int N, int K, float alpha, float beta) { int total_tiles_m = (M + 31) / 32; int total_tiles_n = (N + 31) / 32; int tile_idx = blockIdx.x; // 1D grid int group = tile_idx / (total_tiles_m * total_tiles_n); int local_tile = tile_idx % (total_tiles_m * total_tiles_n); int tile_m = local_tile / total_tiles_n; int tile_n = local_tile % total_tiles_n; if (group >= gridDim.z) return; // we can use 1D grid with z handled by division int m_start = tile_m * 32; int n_start = tile_n * 32; int m_end = min(m_start + 32, M); int n_end = min(n_start + 32, N); // pointers for this group const half* A_g = A + group * M * K; const half* B_g = B + group * N * K; const half* C_g = C + group * M * N; half* D_g = D + group * M * N; __shared__ half smem_A[32 * 64]; // actually we can use smaller __shared__ half smem_B[32 * 64]; // wait, tile is 32x32 but K_chunk can be 64. // A_smem: 32 x 64 // B_smem: 32 x 64 // Actually we can make K_chunk = 64. Then 4 k_sub steps per k_step, 4 k_steps total (K=256). __shared__ float smem_acc[32 * 32]; ``` Wait, with block size 32, shared memory is allocated per block. With 960 blocks, total shared memory usage is 960 * (32*64*2 + 32*32*4) bytes = but it's not all resident. Each SM has 228KB. With 7 blocks per SM, each block can use up to 32KB. Our usage is small. Let's lay out the kernel clearly: ```cuda __global__ void grouped_gemm_wmma_kernel( const half* __restrict__ A, const half* __restrict__ B, const half* __restrict__ C, half* __restrict__ D, int M, int N, int K, float alpha, float beta) { int total_tiles_m = (M + 31) / 32; int total_tiles_n = (N + 31) / 32; int tiles_per_group = total_tiles_m * total_tiles_n; int tile_idx = blockIdx.x; int group = tile_idx / tiles_per_group; int local_tile = tile_idx % tiles_per_group; int tile_m = local_tile / total_tiles_n; int tile_n = local_tile % total_tiles_n; int m0 = tile_m * 32; int n0 = tile_n * 32; const half* A_ptr = A + group * M * K; const half* B_ptr = B + group * N * K; const half* C_ptr = C + group * M * N; half* D_ptr = D + group * M * N; __shared__ half smem_A[32 * 64]; __shared__ half smem_B[32 * 64]; __shared__ float smem_acc[32 * 32]; // initialize accumulator for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < 32*32; i += 32) { smem_acc[i] = 0.0f; } __syncthreads(); // though with 1 warp, not strictly needed for this loop if we handle it right, but safe. wmma::fragment<wmma::accumulator, 16, 16, 16, float> acc[4]; for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) wmma::fill_fragment(acc[i], 0.0f); const int K_chunk = 64; for (int k_step = 0; k_step < (K + K_chunk - 1) / K_chunk; ++k_step) { int k0 = k_step * K_chunk; // Load A tile [32, K_chunk] to smem_A // each thread loads 64 elements (3200 total, 32 threads -> 100 loops per thread? No: 32*64=2048 elements. 32 threads -> 64 elements each.) // Actually 32*64 = 2048 halfs. 32 threads -> 64 halfs per thread. #pragma unroll for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < 32 * K_chunk; i += 32) { int m_local = i / K_chunk; int k_local = i % K_chunk; int m = m0 + m_local; int k = k0 + k_local; half val = __float2half(0.0f); if (m < M && k < K) val = A_ptr[m * K + k]; smem_A[i] = val; } // Load B tile [32, K_chunk] to smem_B (transposed from [N, K] to [K_chunk, N] col-major) // Wait, B_ptr is [N, K] row-major. For smem_B we want [K_chunk, N] col-major. // In smem_B, element (k, n) at k + n*K_chunk. // In GMEM B, element (n, k) at n*K + k. // So for i = k + n*K_chunk, n = i/K_chunk, k = i%K_chunk. #pragma unroll for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < 32 * K_chunk; i += 32) { int n_local = i / K_chunk; int k_local = i % K_chunk; int n = n0 + n_local; int k = k0 + k_local; half val = __float2half(0.0f); if (n < N && k < K) val = B_ptr[n * K + k]; smem_B[i] = val; } __syncthreads(); // WMMA sub-steps wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_a, 16, 16, 16, half, wmma::row_major> frag_a[2]; wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_b, 16, 16, 16, half, wmma::col_major> frag_b[2]; #pragma unroll for (int k_sub = 0; k_sub < K_chunk; k_sub += 16) { // load A frags wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_a[0], smem_A + 0 * K_chunk + k_sub, K_chunk); // rows 0..15 wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_a[1], smem_A + 16 * K_chunk + k_sub, K_chunk); // rows 16..31 // load B frags wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_b[0], smem_B + k_sub + 0 * K_chunk, K_chunk); // cols 0..15 wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_b[1], smem_B + k_sub + 16 * K_chunk, K_chunk); // cols 16..31 // mma wmma::mma_sync(acc[0], frag_a[0], frag_b[0], acc[0]); // (0,0) wmma::mma_sync(acc[1], frag_a[0], frag_b[1], acc[1]); // (0,1) wmma::mma_sync(acc[2], frag_a[1], frag_b[0], acc[2]); // (1,0) wmma::mma_sync(acc[3], frag_a[1], frag_b[1], acc[3]); // (1,1) } __syncthreads(); } // store acc to smem_acc wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + 0 * 32 + 0, acc[0], 32, wmma::mem_row_major); wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + 0 * 32 + 16, acc[1], 32, wmma::mem_row_major); wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + 16 * 32 + 0, acc[2], 32, wmma::mem_row_major); wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + 16 * 32 + 16, acc[3], 32, wmma::mem_row_major); __syncthreads(); // epilogue #pragma unroll for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < 32 * 32; idx += 32) { int m_local = idx / 32; int n_local = idx % 32; int m = m0 + m_local; int n = n0 + n_local; if (m < M && n < N) { float val = smem_acc[idx]; if (beta != 0.0f) { val = alpha * val + beta * __half2float(C_ptr[m * N + n]); } else { val = alpha * val; } D_ptr[m * N + n] = __float2half(val); } } } ``` This looks solid! Let's use 64x64 tiles with 4 warps (128 threads). - smem_A: 64*64 half - smem_B: 64*64 half - smem_acc: 64*64 float - Total: 32KB. Grid: 240 blocks. For each 64x64 tile: - Warp 0: m=0..31, n=0..31 (2x2 WMMA tiles). - Warp 1: m=0..31, n=32..63. - Warp 2: m=32..63, n=0..31. - Warp 3: m=32..63, n=32..63. In each warp: - m_sub within warp: 0 or 16. - n_sub within warp: 0 or 16. - But warp 1 has n_offset = 32, so n_sub = 32 or 48. - warp 2 has m_offset = 32, so m_sub = 32 or 48. - warp 3 has both. ```cuda int warp_id = threadIdx.x / 32; int warp_m = warp_id / 2; // 0 or 1 int warp_n = warp_id % 2; // 0 or 1 int m_base = warp_m * 32; int n_base = warp_n * 32; ``` Inside k_sub loop: ```cuda for (int k_sub = 0; k_sub < 64; k_sub += 16) { wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_a[0], smem_A + (m_base + 0) * 64 + k_sub, 64); wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_a[1], smem_A + (m_base + 16) * 64 + k_sub, 64); wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_b[0], smem_B + k_sub * 64 + (n_base + 0), 64); wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_b[1], smem_B + k_sub * 64 + (n_base + 16), 64); mma(acc[0], frag_a[0], frag_b[0], acc[0]); // (m_base+0, n_base+0) mma(acc[1], frag_a[0], frag_b[1], acc[1]); // (m_base+0, n_base+16) mma(acc[2], frag_a[1], frag_b[0], acc[2]); // (m_base+16, n_base+0) mma(acc[3], frag_a[1], frag_b[1], acc[3]); // (m_base+16, n_base+16) } ``` Wait, `wmma::load_matrix_sync` for matrix_b with row-major expects the pointer to the first row, and `ldm` is the number of elements per row. So for a tile at row `k_sub`, column `n_base`, the address is `smem_B + k_sub * 64 + n_base`. Correct. And for matrix_a with row-major, pointer to row `m_base + tm*16`, column `k_sub`: `smem_A + (m_base + tm*16) * 64 + k_sub`. Correct. Great. Now for GMEM copy: A_gmem layout: row-major [M, K]. Element (m, k) at m*K + k. B_gmem layout: row-major [K, N]. Element (k, n) at k*N + n. Copy loops: ```cuda // A_smem is row-major [64, 64] for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < 64*64; i += blockDim.x) { int m_local = i / 64; int k_local = i % 64; int m = m0 + m_local; int k = k0 + k_local; smem_A[i] = (m < M && k < K) ? A_g[m * K + k] : __float2half(0.0f); } // B_smem is row-major [64, 64] for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < 64*64; i += blockDim.x) { int k_local = i / 64; int n_local = i % 64; int k = k0 + k_local; int n = n0 + n_local; smem_B[i] = (k < K && n < N) ? B_g[k * N + n] : __float2half(0.0f); } ``` Note that for B, `i/64` gives the row (k_local) and `i%64` gives the column (n_local). This loads k rows first. In GMEM, for fixed k, n is contiguous. So threads with consecutive i have consecutive n, which is coalesced. Good. Wait, `i/64` increments every 64 elements. So threads 0..63 load row 0, n=0..63. Thread 0 loads n=0, thread 1 loads n=1, etc. This is fully coalesced. Thread 64 loads row 1, n=0. Also coalesced (next row). For A, `i/64` gives m_local, `i%64` gives k_local. So threads load m rows. For fixed m, k is contiguous in GMEM. Coalesced. Perfect. Epilogue: ```cuda for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < 64*64; i += blockDim.x) { int m_local = i / 64; int n_local = i % 64; int m = m0 + m_local; int n = n0 + n_local; if (m < M && n < N) { float val = smem_acc[i]; if (beta != 0.0f) { val = alpha * val + beta * __half2float(C_g[m * N + n]); } else { val = alpha * val; } D_g[m * N + n] = __float2half(val); } } ``` This is straightforward. One issue: `wmma::store_matrix_sync` writes 16x16 floats. With 4 warps, each warp writes to a distinct region of smem_acc. The address for a tile at (m_base + tm*16, n_base + tn*16) is: `smem_acc + (m_base + tm*16) * 64 + (n_base + tn*16)`. `ldm = 64`. So: ```cuda wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + m_base * 64 + n_base, acc[0], 64, wmma::mem_row_major); wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + m_base * 64 + (n_base + 16), acc[1], 64, wmma::mem_row_major); wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + (m_base + 16) * 64 + n_base, acc[2], 64, wmma::mem_row_major); wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + (m_base + 16) * 64 + (n_base + 16), acc[3], 64, wmma::mem_row_major); ``` After all warps store, `__syncthreads()`. Then epilogue. Now, the model should match the hidden reference exactly. The hidden reference calculates `D = alpha * A @ B + beta * C`. Our kernel does the same. Let me draft the full code for submission attempt. ```python import torch import torch.nn as nn from torch.utils.cpp_extension import load_inline cuda_source = r''' #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_fp16.h> #include <mma.h> using namespace nvcuda; __global__ void __launch_bounds__(128) grouped_gemm_wmma_kernel( const half* __restrict__ A, const half* __restrict__ B, const half* __restrict__ C, half* __restrict__ D, int M, int N, int K, float alpha, float beta) { int tiles_m = (M + 63) / 64; int tiles_n = (N + 63) / 64; int tiles_per_group = tiles_m * tiles_n; int block_id = blockIdx.x; int group = block_id / tiles_per_group; int local_tile = block_id % tiles_per_group; int tile_m = local_tile / tiles_n; int tile_n = local_tile % tiles_n; int m0 = tile_m * 64; int n0 = tile_n * 64; const half* A_g = A + (int64_t)group * M * K; const half* B_g = B + (int64_t)group * K * N; const half* C_g = C + (int64_t)group * M * N; half* D_g = D + (int64_t)group * M * N; __shared__ half smem_A[64 * 64]; __shared__ half smem_B[64 * 64]; __shared__ float smem_acc[64 * 64]; // Zero accumulator SMEM (for epilogue path, but we'll overwrite via wmma store) for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < 64 * 64; i += blockDim.x) { smem_acc[i] = 0.0f; } __syncthreads(); wmma::fragment<wmma::accumulator, 16, 16, 16, float> acc[4]; for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { wmma::fill_fragment(acc[i], 0.0f); } int K_chunk = 64; int num_k_steps = (K + K_chunk - 1) / K_chunk; for (int k_step = 0; k_step < num_k_steps; ++k_step) { int k0 = k_step * K_chunk; // Load A tile to smem_A (row-major [64, K_chunk]) for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < 64 * K_chunk; i += blockDim.x) { int m_local = i / K_chunk; int k_local = i % K_chunk; int m = m0 + m_local; int k = k0 + k_local; half val = __float2half(0.0f); if (m < M && k < K) { val = A_g[m * K + k]; } smem_A[i] = val; } // Load B tile to smem_B (row-major [K_chunk, 64]) for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < K_chunk * 64; i += blockDim.x) { int k_local = i / 64; int n_local = i % 64; int k = k0 + k_local; int n = n0 + n_local; half val = __float2half(0.0f); if (k < K && n < N) { val = B_g[k * N + n]; } smem_B[i] = val; } __syncthreads(); int warp_id = threadIdx.x / 32; int lane = threadIdx.x % 32; int warp_m = warp_id / 2; int warp_n = warp_id % 2; int m_base = warp_m * 32; int n_base = warp_n * 32; wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_a, 16, 16, 16, half, wmma::row_major> frag_a[2]; wmma::fragment<wmma::matrix_b, 16, 16, 16, half, wmma::row_major> frag_b[2]; #pragma unroll for (int k_sub = 0; k_sub < K_chunk; k_sub += 16) { wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_a[0], smem_A + (m_base + 0) * K_chunk + k_sub, K_chunk); wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_a[1], smem_A + (m_base + 16) * K_chunk + k_sub, K_chunk); wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_b[0], smem_B + k_sub * 64 + (n_base + 0), 64); wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_b[1], smem_B + k_sub * 64 + (n_base + 16), 64); wmma::mma_sync(acc[0], frag_a[0], frag_b[0], acc[0]); wmma::mma_sync(acc[1], frag_a[0], frag_b[1], acc[1]); wmma::mma_sync(acc[2], frag_a[1], frag_b[0], acc[2]); wmma::mma_sync(acc[3], frag_a[1], frag_b[1], acc[3]); } __syncthreads(); } // Store accumulators to smem_acc int warp_id = threadIdx.x / 32; int m_base = (warp_id / 2) * 32; int n_base = (warp_id % 2) * 32; wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + m_base * 64 + n_base, acc[0], 64, wmma::mem_row_major); wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + m_base * 64 + (n_base + 16), acc[1], 64, wmma::mem_row_major); wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + (m_base + 16) * 64 + n_base, acc[2], 64, wmma::mem_row_major); wmma::store_matrix_sync(smem_acc + (m_base + 16) * 64 + (n_base + 16), acc[3], 64, wmma::mem_row_major); __syncthreads(); // Epilogue: D = alpha * acc + beta * C for (int i = threadIdx.x; i < 64 * 64; i += blockDim.x) { int m_local = i / 64; int n_local = i % 64; int m = m0 + m_local; int n = n0 + n_local; if (m < M && n < N) { float val = smem_acc[i]; if (beta != 0.0f) { val = alpha * val + beta * __half2float(C_g[m * N + n]); } else { val = alpha * val; } D_g[m * N + n] = __float2half(val); } } } torch::Tensor grouped_gemm_forward( torch::Tensor A, torch::Tensor B, torch::Tensor C, float alpha, float beta) { int groups = A.size(0); int M = A.size(1); int K = A.size(2); int N = B.size(2); auto D = torch::empty({groups, M, N}, A.options()); int tiles_m = (M + 63) / 64; int tiles_n = (N + 63) / 64; int total_blocks = groups * tiles_m * tiles_n; if (total_blocks > 0) { grouped_gemm_wmma_kernel<<<total_blocks, 128>>>( reinterpret_cast<const half*>(A.data_ptr<at::Half>()), reinterpret_cast<const half*>(B.data_ptr<at::Half>()), reinterpret_cast<const half*>(C.data_ptr<at::Half>()), reinterpret_cast<half*>(D.data_ptr<at::Half>()), M, N, K, alpha, beta); } return D; } PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) { m.def("forward", &grouped_gemm_forward, "Grouped GEMM WMMA forward"); } ''' module = load_inline( name='grouped_gemm_h100', cuda_sources=cuda_source, functions=['forward'], extra_cuda_cflags=['-O3', '--use_fast_math', '-gencode', 'arch=compute_90,code=sm_90', '-std=c++17'], verbose=False, ) GROUPS = 15 M = 256 N = 256 K = 256 ALPHA = 1.0 BETA = 0.0 class ModelNew(nn.Module): def __init__(self, groups, k, n, alpha, beta): super().__init__() self.alpha = alpha self.beta = beta torch.manual_seed(3080) self.register_buffer('weight', torch.randn(groups, k, n, dtype=torch.float16, device='cuda')) if beta != 0.0: torch.manual_seed(3081) self.register_buffer('bias', torch.randn(groups, M, n, dtype=torch.float16, device='cuda')) else: self.register_buffer('bias', torch.empty(0, dtype=torch.float16, device='cuda')) def forward(self, x): x_fp16 = x.half() if self.bias.numel() > 0: C = self.bias else: C = torch.empty(self.weight.size(0), x_fp16.size(1), self.weight.size(2), dtype=torch.float16, device=x.device) return module.forward(x_fp16, self.weight, C, self.alpha, self.beta) ``` I need to verify compilation. Copy loop: ```cuda int m_local = i / K_chunk; // i / 64 int k_local = i % K_chunk; // i % 64 int m = m0 + m_local; int k = k0 + k_local; val = A_g[m * K + k]; smem_A[m_local * 64 + k_local] = val; ``` And `wmma::load_matrix_sync(frag_a, smem_A + (m_base + tm*16) * 64 + k_sub, 64)`: Points to row `m_base + tm*16`, column `k_sub`. Correct. Everything looks good. Wait, in the copy loop for smem_A, I wrote `smem_A[i] = val` in my head, but the actual index should be `m_local * 64 + k_local`. However, since `i` goes from 0 to 4095 in order, and `m_local * 64 + k_local` for `m_local = i/64, k_local = i%64` is exactly `i`. So `smem_A[i]` is fine! The layout is naturally linear row-major. Same for `smem_B[i]`. So the copy loops can just use `smem_A[i]` and `smem_B[i]`. Good. Now, compile. Oh wait, I should double check `wmma::fragment` includes for CUDA 12.x (which is on H100). The include `<mma.h>` should be there. Also, `__half2float` and `__float2half` are built-ins. Let me test compile.
call → compile_kernel
